| D009 | Explicit module AIV metadata and explicit pb32 predicate granularity are required. | P0 CONTRACT PROPOSAL, **carrier DECISIVE and MANDATORY; omission is SILENT at the Golden's geometry** (sharpened 2026-10-13) | HIVMC dispatch uses module_core_type (`hivm.module_core_type<AIV>`, `HIVMAttrs.td:317-330`). Discriminator re-read and mechanism re-derived from source this session: `AVEPgePattern` (`AVENormalizeOps.cpp:587-631`) reads `functionType` via `getBitWidthFromAttr` (`Utils.cpp:864-884`); if absent it derives `VL_BITS/numElems` (2048/numElems, `Utils.h:567,577-578`) and accepts only 8/16/32, else `report_fatal_error` (`:625`). Input `pge <ALL> {functionType = #ave.func_dist_type<pb32>} : vector<256xi1>` -> `intr.hivm.pge.b32 {mask_bit_width = 32}`; absent -> `pge.b8 {mask_bit_width = 8}`. **The Golden mask is `boolx256` (`golden_codegen_input.tir:30`): 2048/256 = 8 is a LEGAL derived width, so omitting the carrier produces silent `pge.b8` wrong-code, not a diagnostic.** (A 32-element mask would derive 64 and abort — see PG-03 rc134 — so a smaller test geometry would have caught this; the Golden will not.) Caveat: the canonical template `arith-constant-op-to-hivm-regbase-broadcast.mlir:20` writes `pge <ALL> : vector<64xi1>` with NO carrier and would silently derive 32 — actively misleading, do not copy. **Corroborating constant (Lead, 2026-10-13): `Utils.h:578` declares `constexpr static unsigned PREDICATE_BITS = 256`, and the Golden's mask is `T.boolx256` — the predicate is exactly `PREDICATE_BITS` wide, i.e. 32 bits per element, which is what `pb32` states and what the `b8` fallback contradicts.** The `b8` fallback is therefore not a harmless alternative encoding but a value that contradicts the declared predicate width. Evidence: `lead/lead-source-trace.md` §20, §23, §28; `p1-20261013-late-kernel/lead/diagnostics/README.md`. P1 obligation: assert emitted `mask_bit_width = 32` directly (B5–B7); compile success is not evidence. |

| D012 | Do not map Golden store cache4 silently into a cache0-only high-level store path. | **RESOLVED by user adjudication 2026-10-13 (D021): option (a), a narrow store-side cache carrier extension. HARD GATE retained until the extension lands.** Carrier search EXHAUSTED + SILENT-DROP PROVEN 2026-10-13 | Golden TIR:43 and TileLang builtin.h/codegen_ascend.cc forward cache4. Exhaustive search found NO MTE3 store-side cache carrier in the HIVM dialect: `StoreOp` has no cache operand (`HIVMDMAOps.td:157`); `HIVM_EvictionPolicyAttr` is `LoadOp`-only (MTE2, `:101`); raw `l2_cache_mode` I32Attr is `ND2NZOp`-only (MTE2, `:396`); the store libcall lowering appends only `atomicKind` (`HIVMToStandard.cpp:1020-1032`); the C310 template hardcodes `l2_cache_ctl=0` (`DMAUtils.h:277`, identical in both candidate paths). **New, decisive:** probe SD-03 sets `{l2_cache_ctl = 4 : i64}` on `hivm.hir.store`; it **parses without error** but `-convert-hivm-to-std` output is byte-identical to the cache0 case SD-01 apart from the function name — the libcall still receives `%c0_i32`. The attribute is therefore **silently discarded**, not rejected. **User decision:** implement option (a) — a reviewed narrow ODS/attribute extension on the store side plus the matching lowering change, so the emitted IR carries cache4 rather than dropping it. Options (b) equivalence and (c) different op were not chosen; (c) was already subsumed by (a) since the carrier search found no existing op. **Obligation:** the extension must be independently reviewed, and P1 must assert the cache4 carrier is present in the emitted IR — a clean compile is not evidence. Numerical equality alone cannot waive this. **Mechanism fully explained 2026-10-13 (Lead, read-only, `p1-20261013-late-kernel/lead/p1-lead-spotchecks.md` §2a), which makes the extension concrete rather than invented:** the drop is doubly determined — `StoreOp` declares **no cache field of any kind** (`HIVM/IR/HIVMDMAOps.td:184-186`; `{l2_cache_ctl = 4}` parses only because the assembly format's `attr-dict` accepts discardable attributes), **and** the store template **hardcodes** `uint8_t l2_cache_ctl = 0;` on the `__DAV_C310__` path (`lib/Template/include/RegBase/DMAUtils.h:277`). The shape of the fix is therefore source-supported: mirror the raw-value attribute `ND2NZOp` already carries (`OptionalAttr<I32Attr>:$l2_cache_mode`, `HIVMDMAOps.td:396`) onto `StoreOp` and thread it into `store_ubuf_to_gm_intrin_core` in place of the hardcoded `0`. The value spaces differ and this must not be conflated: `LoadOp`'s cache exposure is a narrowed 3-value enum `eviction_policy` (`HIVMDMAOps.td:93`; `EvictNormal=1/EvictFirst=2/EvictLast=3`, `HIVMAttrs.td:1203-1205`) while the Golden's `4` is outside that enum, consistent with the op docs' statement that the underlying descriptor field is wider than the enum (`HIVMDMAOps.td:381-385`). **P1 does not implement the extension**; P1 records the absence so it is visible. Evidence: `lead/lead-source-trace.md` §17; `p0v2/logs/SD-01.out`, `SD-03.out`. |
| D013 | Terminal DMA completion is an explicit acceptance requirement; do not assume LLVM return adds a barrier. | **RESOLVED by user adjudication 2026-10-13 (D021): option 2 — a discriminating experiment BEFORE P1 starts. EXPERIMENT REPORTED 2026-10-13; D013 GATE CLOSED (device-layer question carried to P3).** Mechanism re-derived from source 2026-10-13; experiment frozen the same day; first pass executed, triaged, and reported the same day | **Mechanism (re-derived from local source 2026-10-13, superseding the earlier flat citation).** The terminal `PIPE_ALL` barrier is a **GraphSyncSolver artifact, not a legalizer artifact**: `CodeGenerator::insertBarrierAllBeforeReturn` (`GraphSyncSolver/SyncSolverCodeGen.cpp:923-933`) inserts it before the return of a **device-entry** function, and is called only under `options.isIntraCoreMode()` (`:975-982`) — always true for the hardcoded `INTRA_CORE_SYNC` the pass builds (`GraphSyncSolver.cpp:76`; `Utility.h:150-153`). `isDeviceEntry` requires the `hacc.entry` attribute (`HACC/IR/HACCInterfaces.cpp:151-154`; `HACC/IR/HACCAttrs.td:75`), and the pass returns early for `hivm.vector_function` (`GraphSyncSolver.cpp:70-72`; attr `HIVM_VFAttr`, `HIVMAttrs.td:1315`). `InjectSync` adds the same barrier in `BARRIERALL` mode (`InjectSync.cpp:50-67`, `:56`) and **also in `NORMAL` mode**, via `SyncAnalyzer::InsertLastPipeAll` (`InjectSync/SyncAnalysis.cpp:103-119`, called from `Plan()` at `:53-54` with `insertBarAllAtLast` defaulting to **true** at `InjectSync/SyncAnalysis.h:56`), gated on the same `isDeviceEntry`. **The terminal barrier therefore has three emitters: GSS, `InjectSync` `BARRIERALL`, and `InjectSync` `NORMAL`.** The regbase A5 stage selects GSS when `enableHIVMGraphSyncSolver && !enableHIVMInjectBarrierAllSync` (`HIVMRegbasePipelines.cpp:115-131`), which default **true / false** in `bishengir-compile` (`Options.td:202-206`, `:576-579`), so GSS is the normal source; CCE-level `--cce-auto-sync=bar-all` is off by default (`Tools/hivmc/Config.h:534-536,996`). Upstream `ReturnOpLowering` maps `func.return` to `LLVM::ReturnOp` with no barrier (`third-party/llvm-project/mlir/lib/Conversion/FuncToLLVM/FuncToLLVM.cpp:678-719`). **The Late cut skips GSS and InjectSync, so it drops the epilogue and the conversion does not restore it.** **Correction:** the frozen `ascend_reference.asc` is AscendC **source** (1848 bytes of `__simd_vf__`/`__global__` C++), not compiler output, so its lack of a terminal barrier is **not** evidence about emitted code; the earlier "the AscendC path shares the question, so the device layer likely supplies it" inference is weaker than first recorded and is not relied on. **User decision:** run a discriminating experiment with the existing compiler BEFORE P1 production work, rather than carrying the open item into P1 (option 1) or adding an unconditional barrier that the AscendC reference does not have (option 3). **Frozen definition:** `lead/d013-experiment-frozen-20261013.md` — 7 arms (baseline, GSS, inject-normal, inject-barrier-all, GSS-without-`hacc.entry`, GSS-with-`vector_function`, to-LLVM) over one fixture, predictions recorded before execution. **Obligation:** the four Golden flag/wait ops must be preserved verbatim regardless of the outcome; the experiment's result decides whether P1 adds a terminal barrier; the device/runtime layer stays a P3 question. **First-pass outcome (2026-10-13, Lead triage in `lead/d013-triage-20261013.md`):** A1/A2/A5/A6 confirmed as predicted; **A2 confirms GSS inserts the terminal barrier**; **A3 refutes frozen prediction F11** — `NORMAL`-mode `InjectSync` *also* inserts the terminal barrier (site missed: `SyncAnalyzer::InsertLastPipeAll`, `SyncAnalysis.cpp:103-119`), so the barrier has three emitters, all skipped by the Late cut; A4 was `NOT_RUN` (malformed bare invocation printed `--help`) and A7 was `NOT_RUN` (missing `-convert-hivm-to-std` prerequisite). **Final outcome (2026-10-13, `lead/d013-result-20261013.md`):** A1 PASS, A2 PASS, A3 **prediction refuted / result genuine**, A4 **NOT_RUN** (the tool silently swallows the `{…}` form; the frozen arm was not re-frozen and stays NOT_RUN), A5 PASS, A6 PASS, A7 PASS. Two **post-freeze** arms were added, each with its prediction frozen before it ran: **A4-fix PASS** (the corrected `-pass-pipeline=` spelling emits 12 `PIPE_ALL`, last before `return` — confirms F10) and **A8 PASS** (GSS → `-convert-hivm-to-std` → `-convert-hivm-to-llvm` preserves exactly one `hivm.intr.hivm.BARRIER` with `pipe = 6` immediately before `llvm.return`). §5 row `HAS / NO / NO` — **DROP CONFIRMED**. **A7 plus A8 together sharpen the mechanism:** the conversion **adds no** barrier (A7) and **preserves one** a sync pass created (A8), so the Late cut loses the epilogue only because no pass creates it there. **Run-3 process incident (disclosed by the executor, independently re-verified by the Lead):** a per-run selector `D013_ONLY` was passed as an environment variable, but `scripts/in-container.sh` forwards only `HOME` and `HIVM_CONDA_ENV` to `docker exec`, so it was silently dropped and **all arms re-executed, including A3, which the Lead had forbidden re-running**. The Lead re-verified that no evidence value changed (A3's observed string and `FAIL` verdict identical in all three runs; A1/A2/A4/A5/A6/A7 identical run 2 vs run 3; `logs/A7.out` byte-identical to `logs/A7.run2.out`; the fixture still hashes to the frozen spec §3 value) and recorded the breach rather than accepting it silently. A guard was added inside `verify/d013/` (an arm with a row is never re-run unless named in `D013_RERUN`). **General hazard for P1/P2: any per-run selector must be carried by a file or an explicitly forwarded variable.** **D013's closure condition is met: the experiment reported.** **P1 obligation:** the Late IR tail must carry terminal completion explicitly, or the drop must be justified by independent device-layer evidence; the four Golden flag/wait ops are preserved verbatim. **Device/runtime completion is carried forward as an explicit P3 obligation.** Evidence: `lead/lead-source-trace.md` §18; `lead/d013-experiment-frozen-20261013.md`; `lead/d013-triage-20261013.md`; `lead/d013-result-20261013.md`. |

| D018 | `--enable-direct-hivm-lowering` does NOT bypass `buildLowerAVEPipelines` in the `bishengir-compile` device path; the earlier "may already express the needed bypass" hypothesis is FALSIFIED. | **FALSIFIED HYPOTHESIS, 2026-10-13** (supersedes the corresponding sentence in `pass-cut.md` §2 and status.md) | The flag exists in two structs. `hivmave::HIVMAVEPipelineOptions::enableDirectHIVMLowering` (`HIVMAVE/Pipelines/Passes.h:142`) is read by the AVE guard (`HIVMAVEPipelines.cpp:39`) but has **no assignment anywhere** in `bishengir/`; `setupHIVMAVEPipelineOptions` (`regbase/PassPipeline.cpp:150-210`) is hand-written and never calls `config.getEnableDirectHIVMLowering()`. The generated `ConfigUtils.cpp.inc:159` does set it, but that include is expanded only in `setupHIVMPipelineOptions` (`regbase/PassPipeline.cpp:103`, `PassPipeline.cpp:62` via `GEN_HIVM_OPTION_SETUP`), so only the HIVM guard (`HIVMRegbasePipelines.cpp:710`) is reachable. The AVE field therefore keeps `cl::init(false)`, the guard is always true, and the AVE optimizer always runs. Evidence: `lead/lead-source-trace.md` §16. Also corrects a methodology error — the first probe used a PATH-resolved binary that is the CANN 9.2 toolkit copy (sha `248b6c09…`), not the local build (sha `71ac4012…`); see D019. **Consequence:** the Late route cannot bypass the AVE optimizer by passing an existing flag. | D020 | Independent Challenger verdict on the Late-IR contract boundary and the pipeline cut point. | **DELIVERED 2026-10-13 (supersedes the interim NOT_RUN record of the same ID)** | The challenger-opus teammate returned a full source-anchored verdict by SendMessage (it is instructed not to write `.md` artifacts, so `review/` stays empty by design, not by failure). **Verdicts: (a) Late-IR contract boundary = REVISE, with P1 advancement BLOCKED** — the boundary (primitive HIVM/HIVMAVE + explicit addresses/sync/VF/metadata before legalization, exactly one A5 HIVMC LLVM owner) is conceptually correct, but D012 cache4, D013 terminal completion, ABI, pb32/VF preservation and execution evidence are unresolved. **(b) Pipeline cut point = BLOCK for the existing `--enable-direct-hivm-lowering` mechanism; REVISE for the architecture** — a dedicated, mutually exclusive Late entry in the existing RegBase driver, no new target kind, skipping normal HIR/HFusion, bufferization, planning, auto-sync, loop lowering, multibuffer and `buildLowerAVEPipelines`, retaining validated legalization and exactly one `runHIVMCCompileA5`. **(c) D018 = option (b)** (plumbing the getter (a) leaves upstream transformations; optimizer-survival (c) is unproven/unsafe). **(d) D012 = BLOCK for P1** — accepted equivalence could be considered only with independent exact-target source/device evidence that cache4 and the emitted cache0 path are semantically equivalent for this exact store and target; numerical equality alone cannot close it. Two new Challenger source claims were re-verified by the Lead: `AppendDeviceSpec` is **not idempotent** (warns "Overwriting the device spec…" then `setNPUTargetSpec` overwrites unconditionally, `AppendDeviceSpec.cpp:142-151`; `setNPUTargetSpec` builds a whole new `TargetSystemSpecAttr`, `HACC/Utils/Utils.cpp:192-202`), and the pge width default also exists on the lowering path (`HIVMPgeOpLowering`, `HIVMAVEToAVEIntrin.cpp:1419-1443`, same `VL_BITS/dstTyNumElems` fallback), independently confirming the §28 D009 mechanism. **An independent verifier check (read-only) CONCURRED with both**, adding: (i) AppendDeviceSpec is non-idempotent exactly when a pre-existing spec differs (hand-set/other-populator, different custom AIC/AIV/HIVMCVersion, option-vs-IR mismatch, regbase-vs-membase entry-set difference) — value-idempotent only on same-pass/same-option reruns; three add sites exist but TTIR/HIR are separate pipelines, so no current driver adds it twice. (ii) `HIVMPgeOpLowering` is where `mask_bit_width` is **stamped** (`:1439-1440`, key `Dialect/Utils/Util.h:75`), and it does **not** validate the derived width itself — `buildPgeOp` (`RegbaseUtils.cpp:604-629`) is the second guard, so the derived-64 abort has two sequential guards while the Golden's derived-8 is silently accepted by both. Evidence: `lead/lead-source-trace.md` §30; `verify/challenger-claims-check.log`. No compiler/hardware run was performed by the Challenger or the verifier; both verdicts are source-review only. |

| D022 | **B8's `dynamic UB=196608` clause was escalated as unsatisfiable; that escalation is WITHDRAWN — the clause is satisfiable and the Lead's premise was wrong.** Raised by the Lead during P1, 2026-10-13; **retracted by the Lead the same day after a discriminating experiment.** | **WITHDRAWN by the Lead, 2026-10-13 — no user adjudication needed; B8 stands as frozen and as written.** | The escalation claimed the Golden's 196608-byte dynamic arena "has no Late-IR carrier" and therefore could not be asserted from the parsed IR. **That premise is false.** The Golden's own prepared TIR carries it as a PrimFunc attribute at the line that defines the Late boundary: `golden_codegen_input.tir:8`, `T.func_attr({..., "dyn_shared_memory_buf": T.int64(196608), "thread_extent": {"blockIdx.x": 64}, ...})`. **The Lead's error was a conflation:** a tree-wide grep of `AscendNPU-IR` for `dyn_shared_memory_buf`/`dyn_shared` returns zero hits, and the Lead read zero *consumers* as zero *carriers*. An attribute no device pass reads still parses and round-trips — the same standing the Lead recorded correctly for `hacc.block_dim` (`p1-lead-spotchecks.md` §5), one section away. **Measurement (one-line patch of the artifact adding `dyn_shared_memory_buf = 196608 : i64` to the entry func attributes, `lead/diagnostics/artifact_plus_arena.mlir`):** bare parse rc=0/stderr 0 with the attribute present; `-ave-normalize-ops -convert-hivmave-to-ave-intrin` rc=0/stderr 0 emitting `pge.b32`/`mask_bit_width = 32`; `-hacc-append-device-spec=... -convert-hivmave-to-ave-intrin` rc=0 with the attribute surviving the append. **Consequences:** (1) the verifier's frozen `B8.UB_ARENA` accepted-key list was right to include `dyn_shared_memory_buf`; the check is satisfiable and must be asserted from the parsed IR, so **no frozen acceptance criterion is reworded and none needed the user's call**; (2) **the artifact gap is now CLOSED** — the delivered artifact carries the attribute (`impl/vadd_late.mlir:127`, `dyn_shared_memory_buf = 196608 : i64`; ## P0 proposal rationale

Current source supports the previously planned backend/runtime reuse and single HIVMC ownership, provided the Late boundary supplies module AIV metadata, static bare-pointer-compatible device parameters and explicit AVE distribution attributes. Exact source syntax and isolated probes are required before final independent review. The full-kernel path, binary ABI and hardware behavior remain later phase gates.

Reverified TileLang source supports one target variant manifest with explicit reuse of the Ascend pipeline, STANDARD_HOST_CODEGENS and tvm_ffi; separate HIVM callbacks belong under backend ownership. The source-only mode is a declared capability, not a fallback path. Future routing tests must check the full truth table, including explicit rejection of pto+hivm. An in-memory Golden target derivative needs semantic comparison with only the reviewed routing change normalized; frozen bytes remain unchanged.

Potential conflict: a physical predicate type alone does not preserve Golden pset32; missing AVE pb32 distribution lowers as predicate8. The smallest discriminator is the same one-op input with and without pb32, using existing compiler only. Expected PgeB32 vs PgeB8 is defined before execution; no Golden change is allowed.

Unknown terminal DMA completion and generated binary ABI are tracked as explicit P1/P2/P3 obligations. If source or experiment requires an architecture change, record the smallest discriminating result before asking the user to decide.

(2) reject it when the value is present but **not 4**; (3) assert the attribute's type is `i32`; (4) keep the existing exactly-one-store check and the whole DMA-shape conjunct unchanged, so B4's current PASS and its exact diagnostics are preserved; (5) each new diagnostic must **name** the defect (the frozen rows key on `l2_cache` / `cache_mode` / `carrier`) and must fire in the preflight, before any transformation; (6) correct the source comment that asserts the opposite, since a load-bearing wrong rationale left in the tree is a landmine for the next reader. **Prohibitions:** no test, fixture, manifest or sealed-suite edit; no touching the P1 preserved bundle, D031's compiler pin, or `build/bin`'s restored P1 identity; a new build must be **re-staged as a new identity** with the superseded `2bf961bf…` recorded in the identity inventory rather than silently replaced. **B1's frozen verdict may not be used as acceptance evidence**, and acceptance after the repair must rest on **B2 flipping to a real named V7 diagnostic** plus a **discriminating supplementary probe**: re-run `b01_cache0.mlir` **without** `--mlir-print-ir-after-all`, so the only possible `l2_cache` source is a validator line — that removes the dump-text confound without touching the sealed oracle (goal §7: the test must fail for the intended semantic assertion, not for a hash or parse reason). **Escalation:** if the implementor still disputes the direction it must come back to the Lead rather than re-implementing the original reading; if repair attempt 1 fails, the Challenger is woken immediately (a focused hard failure), not a self-authorized second attempt. See `docs/status.md` "P2-A 验收裁定". |

**D034 citation correction (Lead, 2026-10-13T16:1xZ) — the cited raw log path no longer holds the bytes this ruling quotes.** The D034 row cites `verify/logs/suite/B2.stderr` (and `B1.stderr:25`) as the FAIL evidence. That directory is the suite runner's **fixed** log dir (`run_p2_suite.py:1725` `log = VERIFY / "logs" / "suite"`), so when the repair-1 re-verification ran it **overwrote those files in place**; `B2.stderr` now shows the **post-repair** V7 diagnostic and reads as if B2 passed. **The ruling is unaffected** — its content was established and recorded before the overwrite, and the pre-repair *shape* is quoted verbatim above (no validator line; the `:544` stub as the only error) — but the citation must not be followed to that path expecting those bytes. **Recovery, in flight:** the superseded bundle is still byte-intact (`impl/stage-p2a/bin/bishengir-compile` = `2bf961bf…`), which is exactly why it was retained, so the Verifier is re-running `b01`/`b02` against it into a clearly-labelled `verify/work/p2a-acceptance/pre-repair-reconstruction/` and will state in-file that these are reconstructions, not the original files. The first run's **summary** did survive, archived at `verify/work/p2a-acceptance/suite-summary.json` (`23:37`), and the pre-repair golden-discriminator evidence survives at `verify/work/p2a-acceptance/discriminator/` (`23:33`). **Process fix:** per-row logs stay in each run's own directory from now on. **This was the Lead's instruction defect, not the Verifier's** — the instruction named a result directory without accounting for the runner's hard-coded per-row log path (same root as the §13 omission). Recorded in `docs/status.md` §17.

The five post-re-affirmation commits (`3349809`, `236c295`, `b258664`, `e4d7f1c`, `d6b02cf`, 148/17) are **envelope-preserving corrections/evidence**, not a new design round; the Challenger named the one boundary that must hold — the pass-route silent-ignore stays a **later §8 step-8 scope question** and must **not** be silently promoted into P2-A implementation scope. **Q3 — the repair brief is neither over- nor under-specified.** **A1 exclusion confirmed correct**: record it NOT_RUN with the deliberate stub limitation; golden preflight success followed by the stub's rc=1 proves *validator acceptance*, not positive binary compilation; **A1 remains mandatory with rc=0 and an independently inspected fresh binary at P2-C/P2-D**. **Two corrections to my own record adopted:** (a) `verifyStoreCacheMode` is **not** exclusively a shape predicate — it checks value, target and shape; §0.7 has been rewritten, because the operative claim is narrower (*neither it nor `StoreOp::verify` requires a present `l2_cache_mode = 4`*) and must not rest on an inaccurate label; (b) the Challenger's verdict is **contract/delta approval only** — it states plainly that it did **not** independently inspect the r1 execution logs, so the 24/24 result remains **Verifier/Lead execution evidence**. **Consequence:** §0.9's design condition is closed; the scoped P2-A acceptance ruling proceeds, gated only on the one supplementary instrument the Challenger named three times (standalone **staged** `bishengir-opt` validator run, no dumps: golden rc=0; b01/b02 rc!=0 with named V7), which the Lead ordered run **before** ruling so the positive stops being an inference from the `:544` stub and §0.4's strict form is discharged. **Instrument-invalid is not P2-A-fails** — an unsupported standalone invocation must be reported as a fact about the instrument, never as a FAIL of any row. |

MLIR's statistics tree was **not characterised** for whether it also names passes that were scheduled but not executed, so its **absence signal is uncharacterised** and it can neither carry the B-row zeros alone nor dissolve A11's `missing_lock`; (2) it is **not** the channel the frozen suite uses — `run_p2_suite.py:733-735` records pass-statistics and explicitly never asserts on it (the in-process HIVMC tail may not emit it) — so it **cannot repair D-b** and is, at most, a candidate for the **additive** remedy in D038; (3) it is genuine pass-manager instrumentation read at runtime, not source-grep counting, so it meets goal §5's evidence-form requirement. **Consequence:** no row is flipped by this entry, no byte of `verify/` changes, and D038's adjudication stays with the Challenger. |

| D040 | **The frozen oracle's wrong-reason guards are case-insensitive substring tests over the whole of stderr, and several of their keys are free tokens; the P2-B boundary-log wording as first written would have guaranteed three of them before any validator ran.** Measured by the Lead 2026-10-14 on the sealed runner and on `verify/work/p2a-acceptance-r1/logs-suite/`; instrument-side wording fix required **pre-commit**; the oracle is not touched. | **RECORDED. Fix required in the instrumentation (free, pre-commit); NO oracle edit; NO row's verdict changes.** | **Mechanism.** `_expect_reject` (`run_p2_suite.py:571-594`) and `_expect_catch` (`:955-981`) choose PASS, on the `rc != 0` branch, by `any(k.lower() in blob for k in diag_keys)` where `blob = err + "\n" + out` — a **substring** test, not a structured diagnostic match. **Free tokens, measured.** All **9** refusal sites in `regbase/LatePipeline.cpp` (`:265, :314, :332, :341, :352, :499, :510, :543, :567, :580`) print `[ERROR] Late mode: …`, and every `Late input validation V# (…) FAILED` message header carries `Late … stage`; therefore on **any** Late-route rejection stderr necessarily contains `late`, and it contains `stage` on any V1/V3 failure. Keys `late` appears in **A3** (`["llvm","stage","late"]`), **A4** (`["aveintrin","regbaseintrins","stage","late"]`) and **B12c**; `stage` in A3/A4; `entry` in **B7** (`["PIPE_ALL","barrier","entry","terminal"]`) and in V3's and V6's messages; `core` in **A5/A6** via V3's `(module/core metadata)` header. **The collision the P2-B instrumentation would have added, measured against the exact key union extracted from the sealed runner:** `[P2-BOUNDARY] late-route stage=late entry=runLateHIVMPipeline` → `entry` (B7), `late` (A3/A4/B12c), `stage` (A3/A4); the four `entry=`/inference logs → `entry` (B7) and, for `inferLayoutOptimization`, `layout` (B4) — but those four sit on the **ordinary** path, which no `_expect_*` row reaches (they all invoke with the Late flag), so **only the A1 routing line is reachable inside a frozen row**. Because it is emitted as the **first statement of the entry, before any validation**, it would have supplied `late`/`stage`/`entry` on every Late negative fixture, converting A3/A4/B7/B12c's guards into rc≠0 tests. **No verdict changes today — measured, not argued.** For every executed row in the r1 run I recorded the **first matching line number**; all are `stderr:2`, the genuine defect-specific diagnostic (A3 `llvm` from `llvm.return`/`llvm.func`; A4 `aveintrin`/`regbaseintrins`; A5 `module_core_type`; A6 `AIC`/`AIV`; B6/B7/B8 `PIPE_ALL`/`barrier`/`entry`/`terminal` from V6, whose text also states the entry-terminal rule explicitly). So this is a **discrimination** weakness, not an unpassable row: it belongs to the D-a…D-d family in mechanism but **not** in kind — no row here is impossible, and none was reported falsely green. **Disposition.** (1) The instrumentation must be reworded so that **no entry-printed line contains, case-insensitively, any frozen key substring** — `late` and `layout` bite the **function names** too (`runLateHIVMPipeline`, `inferLayoutOptimization`), so a `callee=<function>` spelling cannot be used for those two; tag by the `p2-pass-trace.md` row ID instead. The implementor must **measure** the rewritten literals against the key union and report the table empty, in `P2B-REPORT.md`, before committing — this is required now rather than later because the wording hardens into the staged bundle identity at the next build. (2) The oracle is **not** touched: `verify/` is byte-unchanged (`0677be1a…`), consistent with D038. (3) The weakness in the frozen rows is **disclosed** here and in `docs/status.md` §24 追加 2. (4) The Verifier is required to record, per `rc != 0` row, the **first matching `diag_key`, its stderr line number, and that line's verbatim text** — the datum that separates "rejected for the reason the row names" from "rejected and a boilerplate token matched". (5) Referred to the Challenger: whether it considers this a fifth defect requiring a D038 disposition, and whether requiring production instrumentation to avoid an oracle's key substrings is the right direction (the alternative reading — that the oracle's keys are too weak — is defensible but unfixable here, since the oracle is frozen and sealed). |
| D041 | **P2-B r1 host-build incident: the Implementor ran a build ON THE HOST instead of through `scripts/in-container.sh`, contaminating 240 objects and 3 archives of the shared build tree; the repair is complete and measured, but it left two identity problems behind — `build/bin` no longer holds the D031 pins (breaking two frozen path+SHA bindings), and the Verifier's `p2_compiler` binding points at a SUPERSEDED bundle. Ruling: restore `build/bin` AFTER the last build of the phase, bind only to the final bundle, and record the whole incident as a procedural violation with one disclosed anomaly.** | **Measured 2026-10-14 by the Lead from disk; no consent-gated action was used anywhere in the incident or the repair. Build tree HEAD `cfaa59677` with the same 4 dirty files (unchanged); nothing committed; nothing pushed; no source, fixture, oracle, staged bundle or preserved artifact modified (measured, see Evidence).** | **What happened.** `ninja -j 96 bishengir-compile bishengir-opt` was run on the host (`impl/scratch/p2b-r1-host-incident.txt`, 19657 B, 10-14 00:59). The tree is a CONTAINER build, so `.ninja_deps` names container system headers that do not exist on the host: ninja read 4702 edges dirty, executed ~245, and failed at `lib/Support/.../Process.cpp.o` (host glibc 2.31 / libstdc++ 9 vs container glibc 2.39 / libstdc++ 13 → `mallinfo2`, `arc4random` absent). `HFusionOps/LinalgOps/Version.cpp.o` = dependents of regenerated `.inc`), and **nothing deleted was left unbuilt** (`comm -13` empty). **Severity measured, not assumed:** comparing the pre-deletion sizes captured in `p2b-r1-host-touched.txt` against the post-rebuild sizes for the same paths gives **156 changed** (e.g. `Program.cpp.o` 44232→41736, `ELFAttributeParser.cpp.o` 33816→37440) and 99 unchanged — the host outputs were **materially different objects**, so deleting them was necessary and not hygiene. **Disclosed anomaly, deliberately NOT explained:** all 240 object mtimes in the snapshot fall inside a **0.63 s** window (00:54:15.5072879630 → 00:54:16.1392879830), too fast for 240 real compiles even at `-j96`, yet 156 of them differ in content. `ccache` IS configured (`LLVM_CCACHE_BUILD=ON`, launcher `/home/s00653124/Softwares/ccache/ccache`, `CCACHE_CPP2=yes CCACHE_HASHDIR=yes`), which makes a *hit* instant — but a hit returns the cached bytes, which the 156 size changes contradict. **Both candidate explanations are unmeasured; | D042 | **The first P2-B acceptance run reports `PASS=28`, and two of those PASSes are FALSE: `B12a` and `B12b` are satisfied by the P2-B fail-closed message's own wording, not by any diagnosis of their fixtures. Every other clause of this row stands, including the measured datum and the B12a/B12b disposition**; NO oracle edit — `verify/` is byte-unchanged (`run_p2_suite.py` = `0677be1a…`, the freeze of record).** | **The datum, per row.** `B12a.stderr` = **223 B, exactly 2 lines** (`[INFO] Using merged native A5 regbase pipeline`, then the fail-closed `[ERROR]`); `B12a.stdout` = 0 B; the first matching key is `bitcode` at `stderr:2` **and that line is the boilerplate**. `B12b` is byte-identical in shape: 223 B, 2 lines, first key `bitcode` at `stderr:2`, other matching key `identity`. Neither stream contains the words `lib`, `empty`, `missing`, `no such`, `not found` or any path — grep-verified — so **neither row observed anything about an empty `lib/` or a pre-D012 template**. `B12a`'s fixture defect is environmental (empty staged `lib/`), not an IR defect, so the validator legitimately accepts the golden fixture and there is *nothing left in this build* that could diagnose it: the gate that would is §3.3's, which D037 assigned to **P2-C**. Same ground, same disposition as D039's B-rows. **Root cause and why it is not an oracle defect.** The keys `bitcode`/`identity`/`template`/`meta_op` are deliberately broad because the *real* §6.2 gate (P2-C) is what must name them; the defect is that a message **newly authored for P2-B**, whose entire content is *"this build does not implement that gate yet"*, contains the two words the rows key on. A message written to refuse manufactured a green. This is D040's mechanism in its "always fires" form and D040 item (1)'s rule ("no entry-printed line contains, case-insensitively, any frozen key substring") was violated by a literal **outside the collision table the implementor was asked to produce** — that table evidently covered the `[P2-BOUNDARY]` lines only. **Disposition.** (1) Implementor: reword **all eight** P2-added literals so that none carries a frozen key substring, and produce the collision table over the full set — measured collisions today: routing line → `late`, `stage`, `entry`; `inferLayoutOptimization` line → `entry`, `layout`; `inferMixedCV` and `a5-compile` and `ordinary-regbase-pipeline` lines → `entry` (all three on the ordinary path, unreachable inside a Late row, but the table must still be empty); fail-closed line → `late`, `bitcode`, `identity`; tail-failure line → `late`. **The intended effect of the reword is that `B12a`/`B12b` move from PASS to FAIL** — that is unmasking, not regression, and it is the state in which the rows tell the truth about this build. (2) The oracle is **not** touched; no row is relabelled; the FAIL is the honest label for a row that executed and whose condition did not hold. (3) **Corrected accounting for the run of record** (`suite-summary.json` utc `2026-10-13T17:11:50Z`, bindings `p2_compiler` = `stage-p2b` `47e02604…`, i.e. the bundle D041/R1 supersedes): 28 reported PASS = **26 sound + 2 false (`B12a`, `B12b`)**; FAIL = 6 (`A10`, `A11`, `A12`, `C1`, `C2`, `A15`); NOT_RUN = 1 (`C6`, BLOCKED: `build/bin/bishengir-opt` = `4c31167b…` ≠ the frozen `0761c6e6…` — **the first measurement of D041's second identity consequence, exactly as predicted**). (b) `C1`'s FAIL is the executed witness of D038(d): `C1.absent`'s stderr is `b02_cache_carrier_deleted.mlir` rejected by `late-preflight` (`[ERROR] Late mode: input validation failed.`) while `B1`/`B2` PASS by that same rejection — measured, the two rows cannot both pass. **The one genuine open defect at P2-B is `A15`.** Five options are neither rejected nor pinned — `--enable-optimized-metaop`, `--enable-hivm-auto-storage-align`, `--enable-lib-call-no-inline`, `--enable-hivm-unit-flag-sync`, `--enable-preload` — and each of their five sub-runs (2 lines, 223 B) shows the invocation proceeding to the fail-closed block, i.e. silently accepted; `A15.flag0` proves the **mechanism already works** (`--enable-static-bare-ptr=false` is refused *by name*: "pinned to true in Late mode … it is not silently overridden"). Artifacts: `lead/scratch/d040-first-match-scan.py`, `lead/scratch/d040-first-match-logs-suite-p2b.txt`, `lead/scratch/p2-instrumentation.diff` (the 8 added literals, read from the dirty tree, not from a report). |

steps 4–5 stay P2-C. **Q2 REVISE + BLOCK:** the frozen oracle must be repaired by an *explicit correctness-fix amendment* under `verify/ERRATUM-v2-freeze-time.md` §5 — the sealed runner, its hashes, its historical results and its disclosed limitations are PRESERVED; acceptance through the existing oracle is BLOCKED until re-verification. **Q3 REVISE (evidence claim):** the 33 PassIDs / 58 dumps establish *scheduling and selected IR effects*, **not** all body executions and **not** complete-P2 coverage. **Q4 APPROVE two managers, REVISE my E5 recording:** the Mix/Aiv mode is re-resolved **per manager** while `FilterPassesAttr` is checked **per action**, so any doc that places both only at later-manager boundaries is wrong; all 58 archived tree files lack both attributes, which supports an *inferred* Mix for this run, but first-action boundary mode records remain required. **Q5 REVISE the blanket DMA deferral:** the descriptor extent 8192 f32 = **32768 B** is assertable **now** at the tail for all three descriptor chains; only the *emitted template transaction* defers to P2-C — so the verifier's `D5 NOT_RUN` is more deferential than the evidence requires. **Q6:** (a) APPROVE `B1–B7 NOT_RUN` under E4; (b) REVISE the statistics claim — `PassStatistics.cpp:107-137` recursively prints every `pm.getPasses()` entry **regardless of execution** (including passes with empty statistics), `Pass.cpp:883-892` dumps after `runPasses` **even on failure**, `:592-594` stops execution at the first failure while later entries remain printable, and `:516-530` runs the Before-instrumentation outside the action callback so a filter can suppress the body while names and headers still appear ⇒ the 33 PassIDs prove **constructed pipeline structure, not body execution**; D039's and status §24's wording "four actually ran / execution-level confirmation" must be corrected to *constructed Finalize structure*; (c) structural-absence evidence is CONDITIONAL; (d) a statistics-only *additive replacement* of the frozen acceptance is BLOCKED (supplementary corroboration APPROVED), and any additive test whose verdict replaces a broken frozen row follows the same named correctness-fix governance. **Q6 corollary — D037/D039 corrected:** "the tail and A5 pass sets are disjoint" is **literally false**; measured `bishengir/lib/Tools/bishengir-compile/regbase/PassPipeline.cpp:293` and `bishengir/lib/Tools/hivmc/A5/PassPipelineA5.cpp:53` both add `createLowerAffinePass`. The true and load-bearing claim is **`ConvertHIVMToLLVM` ownership is disjoint** (`PassPipelineA5.cpp:52`) — which is the property that actually prevents duplicate lowering. **Q7 REVISE D042's disposition — my eight-literal production rewording is WITHDRAWN as a repair.** `B12a`/`B12b` are an *executed witness of the oracle's own whole-stream-substring discrimination defect* (D040), **not** a compiler-semantic defect in the truthful fail-closed message; the durable correction belongs in the oracle's diagnostic matching, and production wording must never be shaped by the oracle's token list. Rewording is at most optional confound-reduction — never repair, never acceptance evidence. Isolating experiments (unchanged helpers, isolated namespace, no compiler, no oracle edit): `A3` PASS on the generic P2-B stub alone (`late`); `A4` PASS likewise; `B7` PASS on the routing line plus an unrelated output-file error (`entry`); `B7` PASS on an output-file error plus a note-only `PIPE_ALL`. **Fifth defect (measured by me on the archived bytes, not taken from a report):** both frozen header regexes are inert — `DUMP_RE` (`:247`) and `DUMP_WITH_BODY_RE` (`:277`) each match **0 of 58** real `A10.stderr` header lines (the real delimiter is `) //----- //`, slashes *then* dashes, and the regex asks for `\)\s*-+//`), while a delimiter-corrected regex matches **58/58**; and after repairing only the delimiter, `parse_dump_bodies` returns 58 **dicts** (`{"phase","name","arg","body"}`) so `t_a10`'s `rec[3] if len(rec) > 3 else ""` at `:707` raises **`KeyError: 3`** — the guard tests the record's *arity*, not its *type*. Hence A10's E5 per-manager mode scan is inert **twice over** (no records; then unindexable records), which is also why the AMENDMENT-v2 positive-control pairing never fires. **Third false green — `C3`** (my reading of `t_c3:1433-1454`): its rejection branch PASSes on `"Ascend310B3" in blob or "target" in blob.lower()` over the whole stream, where **`target` is free** on any stream echoing `--target`; and the branch's own message concedes the row's stated claim ("old-ABI shape") is *deferred to `$OPT` calibration* — so a PASS is recorded against coverage the row did not exercise, in a build with **zero** ABI lowering. **`C6` only parses fixtures** (`t_c6:1485-1515`: a calibration table run through the pinned P1 `$OPT`), so it measures fixture drift, not the P2 compiler. **Accounting rule (corrects D042):** do **NOT** derive "26 sound PASS" by subtraction — the false-green set is at least {`B12a`, `B12b`, `C3`}, a sound count is not a subtraction result, and `B12a`/`B12b`'s P2-C gate requirements are **NOT_RUN at P2-B**, not PASS. **C1:** preserved as an executed FAIL and a live witness of D-d; accepting the absent carrier to satisfy C1 would violate V7, so the contract correction goes through the same governed process. **A15 — my earlier dispatch is WITHDRAWN and the diagnosis corrected:** measured `LatePipeline.cpp` `kPinnedTrue` = the five options `{name, true, false}` (`:114-120`) and `applyPin` (`:413-450`): 0 occurrences ⇒ inject the canonical spelling; >1 ⇒ reject as a duplicate; requested ≠ pinned ⇒ reject naming the option and both values; **requested == pinned ⇒ ACCEPTED and deliberately not re-injected**. A15's five sub-runs pass the **bare** flag, which a bool `cl::opt` reads as *true* — the pinned value — so they are accepted **by design** and only then reach the fail-closed stub (rc≠0, naming no option), which is why the frozen row reports FAIL. That is a **scope artifact of the same class as A10–A12/C2, not a pin-mechanism failure**, and "add five table entries with measured values" was the wrong instruction. The single sub-run that actually exercises the pin is the asymmetric `--enable-static-bare-ptr=false`, and it PASSes. Required at P2-C: `=false`, duplicate, absent and parsed-**effective-value** probes on the same bound artifact. **Q8 corrections to D041:** the snapshot delta is **153 objects + 3 archives = 156 paths** (not "156 objects"); the window is ≈**0.636 s**; "a hit returns cached bytes, which the size changes contradict" is **false** — host and container invocations legitimately select different results because their preprocessing and included-header sets differ (`ccache MANUAL.md:2101`); the 240 Ninja object edges (64–725 ms, start/end 426–1388 ms) prove **edge executions during the host invocation, not 240 fresh compilations**, so cache retrieval is *plausible but unproven* without cache-event evidence; `touched='./...'` proves **census scope**, not the absence of writes outside the build directory (ccache itself may write outside); and the generated tblgen executables relinked during the repair belong in the dependency closure. 8b: the guarded restoration under the present bindings is APPROVED, but the preserved baseline is **flat suffixed filenames**, so a direct repoint would break executable-relative template discovery — the immutable baseline bundle is the better future instrument and is deferred; restore **after the last build of the phase** and re-check immediately before any baseline use. 8c: an explicit **wrapper-only** execution rule is APPROVED, and D041's closure is revised to *execution-provenance enforcement*. **Consequence: P2-B IS NOT ACCEPTED.** Path forward: (i) the verifier authors the correctness-fix amendment (new runner + new seal; v2 bytes and historical verdicts preserved) with the required controls — generic-stub, unrelated-I/O-error, boundary-only and dump/note-only streams must all FAIL the rejection-reason check, the accepted Golden positive control is retained; (ii) the implementor commits, builds once and stages the **unchanged** production cut (the source cut is approved as it stands — no rewording required); (iii) acceptance is re-run on the final bundle with `p2_compiler` rebound; (iv) the Challenger re-reviews. |

| D044 | **A13's `env` sub-run is a FOURTH false green, and it is a DIFFERENT defect class from D040 — the invocation never establishes the condition it asserts. Plus a calibrated stub canary that finds the whole class in one sweep, and the observation that the false-green labels are *authored, not measured*.** Raised 2026-10-14 by the Lead from archived bytes, during the verifier's own A13 discrimination work. | **MEASURED; folds into the same ERRATUM §5 correctness-fix amendment** | **Measurement (mine, on the archived stream).** `A13.env.stderr` is **223 B**, sha256 `1eb04fd1ae84c0001c723b4cfaae3516e8a0022be7a5d8baefb631b19d263ce1`, rc=1, stdout 0 B — **byte-identical** to `B12a.stderr` and `B12b.stderr`, i.e. it observed nothing but the fail-closed stub. The same compiler on the same fixture with `BISHENGIR_LEGACY_A5_REGBASE` **actually set** produces a **named** diagnostic instead (the verifier's `verify/work/p2b-acceptance/discrim/a13env-withenv.stderr`, 335 B): `[ERROR] Late mode: BISHENGIR_LEGACY_A5_REGBASE is set (value '1') together with --input-ir-stage=late-hivm. … rejected instead of delegated.` ⇒ in the suite's env sub-run **the variable never reached the compiler**, so its rc=1 came from the stub. A13's recorded verdict — `all incompatible sub-runs rejected for the intended reason: [('env', 1), ('pure-simt', 1), ('mixcv', 1)]` (status **PASS**, verbatim from `suite-summary.json`) — is therefore **unsupported for the `env` sub-run**. Its siblings are genuine (`A13.pure-simt.stderr` 358 B and `A13.mixcv.stderr` 424 B each carry an option-specific named rejection: "Late mode rejects the option --pure-simt … It is not silently ignored"), so the defect is specifically `env`. **Why this is a different class:** no matcher repair can make the row honest — the invocation never created the condition it asserts. The production guard is correct and named (my discrim proves it), so this is the frozen oracle's *provisioning*: `scripts/in-container.sh` is a `docker exec` wrapper, and a host-side `os.environ` assignment need not cross into the container; the recorded `.cmd.txt` argv carries no assignment either. **Root cause DETERMINED (Lead, same day, from frozen source — not inferred).** `t_a13:832` *does* pass the variable: `sub("env", env_extra={"BISHENGIR_LEGACY_A5_REGBASE": "1"})`, and `run_ic:217-222` applies it via `env = dict(os.environ); env.update(env_extra); subprocess.run(argv, …, env=env)`. But `argv = ["bash", CFG.ic, binary] + args` runs `scripts/in-container.sh`, which is `exec docker exec -i -u … -e "HOME=$HOME_DIR" -e "HIVM_CONDA_ENV=$ENV_NAME" … bash -c …` — it forwards **only** those two variables, with no `-e` passthrough and no `--env-file`. `docker exec` does not inherit the client's environment, so the assignment stops at the host wrapper: **set but not forwarded.** Blast radius: `env_extra` has exactly **one** call site (A13's `env` sub-run) and `run_p2_suite.py` is the only script under `verify/tests/scripts/` that touches `os.environ`, so the practical impact is one sub-run — but the **mechanism is generic**: `env_extra` is a parameter that *cannot take effect* through this wrapper, i.e. one more guard that cannot fire, and any future row that uses it will silently test nothing. The repair may **not** be a silent wrapper edit: `scripts/in-container.sh` is shared infrastructure on the **production execution path** — it is what the implementor's builds and every P2-C/P2-D run go through — so changing its env surface changes behaviour for everything and needs separate review. The preferred repair is therefore non-invasive: put the assignment *inside* the container command (pass `bash` with `["-c", "BISHENGIR_LEGACY_A5_REGBASE=1 exec <compiler> …"]`, which the wrapper's `exec "$@"` honours), with a wrapper-level allowlist considered only as a separately-reviewed tooling change. Related to Q8c's wrapper-only rule: the wrapper is the sole execution boundary, and its env surface is exactly `HOME` and `HIVM_CONDA_ENV`. **The stub canary.** A byte-identity sweep over every `*.stderr` in `logs-suite-p2b/` found exactly **8** stub-identical rows: `A13.env` (PASS), `A15.flag1`–`flag5` (FAIL), `B12a`, `B12b` (PASS). The PASS rows observed nothing; the five A15 FAILs are **honest** — their reason text ("does not name the option/policy") is literally true — and they independently **confirm** D043's corrected A15 analysis (`kPinnedTrue` pins the five to true, `applyPin` accepts a request equal to the pin, so a *bare* true request is accepted by design and the compile then dies at the stub), which is exactly what the byte evidence now shows. Proposed as a positive instrument: **any row whose captured stream is byte-identical to the known stub observed nothing and may not be read as evidence, whatever its label says** — this is the general detector, of which D042's "223 B / 2 lines" was one instance. **The labels are authored, not measured.** `B12a`'s recorded detail reads "explicit failure naming 'bitcode' with empty bitcode dir" and `B12b`'s reads "rejected rc=1 with template-identity diagnostic"; each names a mechanism (a directory, an identity diagnostic) that appears **nowhere** in the 223 B stream. The strings are composed from the test author's expectation, which is why they read as evidence while carrying none. **Composite rows hide stub sub-runs** — A13 bundled three sub-runs into a single PASS label that named all three. Required of the amendment: (a) fix the `env` sub-run's provisioning so the variable reaches the compiler, and re-run it as a genuine row; (b) add the stub canary as an instrument; (c) require composite rows to report the **per-sub-run** first-matching line and forbid a composite PASS from resting on a stub-only sub-run. **Corpus of false greens is now {`A13.env`, `B12a`, `B12b`, `C3`}**, and `A13.env`'s cause is not a matcher defect at all. |
| D045 | **Independent Lead census of ALL 40 `rc≠0` streams in the archived P2-B run: the stub canary has TWO forms and its discriminating signature is the `[ERROR]` line, never the stream preamble.** D042's sweep classified 17 rows, but it was a **subset presented as a classification** — `A13`, `A14`, `A15`, `C1` were never in it, which is exactly how `A13.env` stayed invisible until the verifier's own disclosure. Census instrument `lead/scratch/complete-rc-census.py` sha256 `8f225f89869ac337800a9b50bc4c51f33d67063696b079726a3af730b52e6629`, output `complete-rc-census.out.txt` sha256 `94e842e55479c6bc7851021fb7a8c3691e09640ae51c795efdd889802fbe5325`. Two consequences for the ERRATUM §5 amendment: (i) the *generic stub* negative control must be defined as **"the fail-closed stub's `[ERROR]` line is the terminal diagnostic"**, not "stream == stub bytes", otherwise an `A10`-style dump-carrying runaway and a `B12a`-style false green cannot be told apart; D044's "byte-identity is the reliable canary" is thereby **superseded, not extended**; (ii) the canary must never test the stub's **first** line, `[INFO] Using merged native A5 regbase pipeline` — every Late-mode run prints it, so a substring test on it matches **everything**. Also measured: `A15` is **composite** (`flag0` NAMED — the asymmetric `--enable-static-bare-ptr` given `false` against a `true` pin, 192 B; `flag1`–`flag5` bare-stub), so its FAIL mixes one real observation with five scope artifacts and the per-sub-run reporting requirement is not satisfied by any row-level label; `C1.absent` (FAIL, 5 825 B) terminates at the **generic** `input validation failed` line of the `B2`/`B4` family — for a row whose subject is an absent file, the recorded reason is not obviously the row's intent, and it too was never in my sweep. | D047 | **P2-B GATE RULING ON D044 (Challenger `review-2`, 2026-10-14T18:27:04Z): the premise-setup defect is APPROVED as a class distinct from D040, A13's recorded composite PASS is BLOCKED, the stub canary is APPROVED as a correctness instrument with scope controls, and a NEW wrapper-propagation control is required before the A13 root cause may be called proven.** Recorded verbatim-in-substance from the Challenger's written ruling; it is the governing text for the A13 repair and the canary's status in the ERRATUM §5 amendment. | **TAXONOMY REVISED · A13 COMPOSITE PASS BLOCKED · CANARY APPROVED AS CORRECTNESS INSTRUMENT · PROPAGATION CONTROL REQUIRED** | **1. The class split.** "D040 concerns an observed stream whose matcher accepts an unrelated token. D044 occurs earlier: the invocation did not establish the condition that the row claims to test. Lead note: prefer the explicit `docker exec -e` form — the wrapper is shared production-path infrastructure and a change to its env surface needs separate review, per D044's own repair constraint. **3. Canary — APPROVED as a correctness instrument, not supplemental corroboration,** because it directly falsifies labels that claim a reason-specific observation while recording only the universal stub. Scope controls, in its words: bind the canonical hash to **the exact compiler/staged artifact and the run**; sweep **every row and composite sub-run stream**; a reason-specific PASS whose complete stream is byte-identical to the canonical stub is **invalidated** (FAIL or INCONCLUSIVE per the amendment's status model); **do not treat a nonstub hash as proof of correctness** — nonstub streams still require defect-specific primary diagnostics; and **re-run the canary whenever the compiler's universal refusal text or the artifact binding changes** (at P2-C the fail-closed stub is replaced by the real tail, so the canary must then report that it matches nothing rather than silently abstain). Lead note: D045's measurement stands alongside this — the stub has a bare 223 B form and a dump-carrying 282 KB form, so the hash is the *binding* and the property "the stub's `[ERROR]` line is the terminal diagnostic" is the *signature*; the 4 HIT-STUB rows (`A10`, `A11`, `A12`, `C2`) are NAMED-rejection reds that a hash-only rule would sweep. **4. It confirms the eight.** The measured stub-identical set is exactly `{A13.env, A15.flag1…flag5, B12a, B12b}` — matching the Lead's D045 census — with `A13.env`/`B12a`/`B12b` **false greens** and the five A15 rows **honest FAILs that independently support the corrected A15 ruling (D043)**. **No "sound PASS" total may be derived by subtraction.** **5. Evidence-derived details — APPROVED as an amendment requirement.** B12a's detail ("explicit failure naming `bitcode` with empty bitcode dir") and B12b's ("template-identity diagnostic") are authored from expectations and interpolate mechanisms absent from the 223 B stream; A13's composite detail claims all three sub-runs were rejected for their intended reasons without per-sub-run evidence. Required: each detail generated from recorded facts — rc, exact stream/diagnostic line, diagnostic category, artifact presence, and identity data where applicable. **Composite rule stated as a gate: one stub-only sub-run must make the composite FAIL or INCONCLUSIVE, never PASS;** a composite PASS requires every sub-run to carry its own evidence-derived intended-reason result. **6. Governance:** all of the above folds into the ERRATUM-v2-freeze-time §5 correctness-fix / effective-oracle amendment with the old suite outputs preserved — it is not an optional addendum, and it applies to D040 as well since both defects produce false greens and are covered by the same re-verification run. |

(c) `bitcode.p1_historical.path` (`…/build/lib/meta_op.aiv.c310.O2.bc`) — documented "COMPARISON ONLY, not the acceptance identity" and its sha still matches (`5e7ccae2…`), so latent only. **Generalisation:** every absolute path in the frozen bindings that lies under the build tree is a baseline the P2 build deterministically pre-empts, whatever sha it declares — D046 named the instance, this names the class. |
| D049 | **The §5 corrective runner `run_p2_suite_v3.py` ships the very delimiter defect it was written to repair — its own changelog asserts a fix that the shipped literals do not implement, so every dump-derived row in it is inert again.** Found 2026-10-14 by the Lead, by `ast`-extracting the literals from the shipped file and applying them to real header bytes (the standard discipline), not by reading the changelog. | **MEASURED ON THE SHIPPED FILE · REPORTED TO THE VERIFIER · v3 MUST NOT BE SEALED AS-IS; P2-B re-verification must not start on it** | **Measurement.** `verify/tests/scripts/run_p2_suite_v3.py`, sha256 `8699d6e122c7fb35be511fd9…`, 79 927 B, mtime `2026-10-14 03:30:30` (mid-write). Its header `:16-19` states: "**1. DUMP_RE / DUMP_WITH_BODY_RE delimiter.** The toolchain prints `) //----- //` (slashes THEN dashes); v2 asked `\)\s*-+//` and matched 0 of 58 real headers, so every dump-derived scan (A10 per-manager E5, positive-control pairing) was INERT. **Corrected form matches 58/58.**" The shipped literals are still the v2 form: `:305` `DUMP_RE = re.compile(r"^//\s*-+//\s*IR Dump After\s+(.+?)\s+\((.+?)\)\s*-+//")` and `:334` `DUMP_WITH_BODY_RE` … `\)\s*-+//\s*$")`. Extracted by `ast` and run against the **58 real header lines** in `verify/work/p2b-acceptance/logs-suite-p2b/A10.stderr`: **`DUMP_RE` 0/58, `DUMP_WITH_BODY_RE` 0/58**; repairing only the trailer to `\)\s*//-+\s*//` gives **29/58** (After-only, correct) and **58/58** (Before|After, correct). Nothing normalizes the line first — `parse_dump_bodies:347` matches the raw `ln`, and the file's only two `.replace(` calls (`:527`, `:998`) are unrelated to headers. Both parsers are live (`parse_pass_streams` at `:732`, `:806`, `:853`; `parse_dump_bodies` at `:761`), so **`positive_control_filter_off:790` still cannot fire**: with an empty record set it returns `('inconclusive', "no pass printed an After header over an IR-unchanged interval (every paired pass changed the IR)")` — a stated cause that an empty evidence set cannot support. **This is the third appearance of one shape.** (i) v2's `DUMP_RE`, sealed and admitted; (ii) the verifier's `exercise_harness_literals.py`, whose transcribed `suite_re` was recorded as "**expect 0** — D-b" rather than corrected, i.e. the defect documented in the instrument that was supposed to exercise it; (iii) now v3, where the **comment records the correct convention and the code below it keeps the wrong one** — so the comment reads as a fix. **The generalisable rule, which is the reason this is a decision and not a typo:** a changelog line is *authored*, not measured. When a corrective amendment lists "what changed", each item must be re-measured **from the shipped file at seal time** — `ast`-extract the literal, apply it to real bytes, record `N/M` — because a repair claim is exactly the kind of statement that reads as verified while being a statement of intent. This instance also settles a question the record had left open: the defect survived a *governed, explicit, itemised* correctness-fix amendment, which means the amendment's own acceptance needs the same literal-exercise evidence as the rows it repairs, not just a list of intentions. |

(B) `bash scripts/in-container.sh bash -c 'BISHENGIR_LEGACY_A5_REGBASE=1 exec printenv BISHENGIR_LEGACY_A5_REGBASE'` ⇒ prints **`1`**, rc=0. (C) `bash scripts/in-container.sh printenv HIVM_CONDA_ENV` ⇒ `s00653124_tilelang`, rc=0. (D) container-side echo: `REGBASE=[]` for A's form and `REGBASE=[1]` for B's. **Why (C) is not optional:** without it, "printenv printed nothing" is equally consistent with the probe never having reached the container at all — an absence needs its own positive control, the same discipline D048 applied to the routing rows. **Mechanism, from `scripts/in-container.sh:14-20`:** `exec docker exec -i -u "$USER_ID" -e "HOME=…" -e "HIVM_CONDA_ENV=…" -w … "$CONTAINER" bash -c '<source CANN set_env; source conda; conda activate; exec "$@">' _ "$@"`. Exactly two variables are forwarded; the inner `exec "$@"` (line 19) is what makes (B) work, because the assignment is then performed by *the container's own shell* before it `exec`s the payload. **Consequences.** (i) **D044's root cause is proven, not inferred.** The mechanism — a host-side assignment cannot arrive — is now measured directly, and the 335 B named env-conflict diagnostic is its *effect*; the two together close the question, and the diagnostic alone did not, which is precisely why D047 required a control. (ii) **The remedy needs no wrapper change.** D044 left open a second branch — a wrapper-level allowlist, "separately reviewed" because `in-container.sh` is shared production-path infrastructure that every build and every P2-C/P2-D run traverses. The measurement closes that branch: the non-invasive in-container form is sufficient, and the wrapper stays untouched. (iii) The `env_extra` parameter's defect is demonstrated at its boundary rather than argued: it sets a host variable that provably cannot arrive, so any row using it tests nothing. (iv) **Ownership:** my run establishes the mechanism as an independent lead measurement, but execution evidence belongs to the Verifier — the amendment must carry a verifier-run reproduction of A–D (all four, control included) before D047's requirement counts as discharged in the record. |
| D051 | **P2-B GATE RULING ON D045/D046 (Challenger `review-2`, 2026-10-14 ~19:48Z): the stub canary is APPROVED but must be used with an explicit two-stage precedence, D046 is APPROVED as a distinct evidence-freshness class, and the C6 by-path remedy is APPROVED.** (a) **Canary precedence, stated as an order of operations, not a choice between tests.** Stage 1 is the *semantic* detector — the universal stub's `[ERROR]` occurs as the **terminal** diagnostic, with no later defect-specific diagnostic and no artifact/identity evidence after it; this catches the dump-carrying form (`A10`/`A11`/`A12` 282 601 B, `C2` 143 721 B) that a bare-stream test cannot. The **byte hash is retained, demoted to its correct role**: exact artifact/run *binding* and independent `STUB-BARE` labelling, calibrated against the bound compiler/staged bundle — never the semantic detector. Classification is by row expectation: a reason-specific negative row that is terminal-stub has any PASS **invalidated**; an `rc=0` positive row that is terminal-stub stays **FAIL/NOT_RUN** — it is a gate that did not execute, not a false green. The ruling explicitly forbids the worn-out shortcut: line presence *anywhere* in the stream does not make a stub, and the preamble-keyed census is D040-shaped and must not be reused. (b) **D046 is a distinct sixth class, not a matcher bug and not a race** — verdict current, row evidence stale; the archived `C6.*` per-row logs must be marked stale/unattributed to that verdict. (c) **C6 by-path: APPROVED as config-only.** Bind C6 to the verified preserved P1 opt (real copy, with any relative lib layout it needs), keep the declared `0761c6e6…` SHA, verify it **from the preserved manifest's own base directory**, execute C6 only after binding, and record `C6 = NOT_RUN` until then. | D052 | **P2-B GATE RULING ON D048 AND ON THE ROUTING EVIDENCE (Challenger `review-2`, 2026-10-14 ~19:48Z): the config-only correction is APPROVED, `baseline_pre_p2_compiler.path` must not be left as a stale build-tree declaration, and MUTUALLY EXCLUSIVE ROUTING IS PROMOTED FROM A DISCLOSED COVERAGE DELTA TO AN AMENDMENT GATE ITEM.** (a) `bishengir_opt.path` is repointed to the preserved P1 opt real copy with the declared SHA retained, verified from the preserved manifest's own base, and C6 executed in a fresh run directory. (b) `baseline_pre_p2_compiler.path` currently points into the mutable build tree while its SHA names the preserved P1 compiler. Current D4 code consumes only the SHA, but a declared path pointing at a *different* artifact is an integrity hazard: repoint it to the preserved pre-P2 compiler (preferred), or mark it explicitly comparison-only/dead **and** prevent any future row from executing it. | D071 | **`p2_compiler` binds to the markerless `stage-p2b` (47e02604, **0** `[P2-BOUNDARY]` sites) while the P2-B artifact of record — the bundle carrying all **6** marker sites, and the one D059 requires the routing evidence to bind to — is `stage-p2b-r1` (ea217b8d), which appears **nowhere** in `p2-bindings.json`; because A1 and A2 are now marker-gated, an acceptance run at the current binding returns both as NOT_RUN-by-instrumentation and the D052(c)/D056(b) routing gate item becomes unsatisfiable — and the NOT_RUN text will read as a correct diagnosis of an incorrect configuration.** Measured by the Lead 2026-10-14 by `grep -a -o … | wc -l` over each staged binary and by reading the binding file: `stage-p2b` 47e02604 → **0** marker sites; `stage-p2b-r1` ea217b8d → **6**; `stage-p2a-r1` 1a3f20f8 → 0; `grep -o 'ea217b8d…' p2-bindings.json` → **no match**, i.e. the P2-B repair bundle is absent from the whole binding surface (the keys are `p2_compiler`→stage-p2b, `superseded_p2_compiler`→stage-p2a, `superseded_p2_compiler_r1`→**stage-p2a-r1**, `baseline_pre_p2_compiler`→`<build>/build/bin`). **The wiring itself is right and is not in question:** `routing_detail` (`run_p2_suite_v3.py:457`) returns NOT_RUN only when `routing_scan` finds no marker anywhere, its message deliberately names "stage-p2b 47e02604… has zero marker sites", mutual exclusion is enforced in both directions (`:461-481`), `t_a1` maps FAIL→FAIL and NOT_RUN→NOT_RUN with the ELF measurement retained either way, and `t_a2` keeps "a real sha regression still FAILs first" ahead of the marker gate — so the tripwire is *intentional instrumentation* and the defect is purely the **binding**, which is exactly the file the v2 manifest note says acceptance-time binding is expected to edit. Disposition: repoint `p2_compiler` to `impl/stage-p2b-r1/bin/bishengir-compile` (ea217b8d…) with its `bin/bishengir-opt` and both `lib/meta_op.aiv.c310.O{0,2}.bc` siblings, rebind `bitcode.aiv_c310_o2_path` to the r1 bundle's `lib/`, record `stage-p2b` 47e02604 as **superseded** (not deleted), and state the four r1 hashes in the acceptance record so the routing evidence binds to the exact staged compiler SHA and the 4/4 bundle hashes D059 requires — **before** any acceptance run, so that no one is tempted to make A1/A2 green by relaxing the marker requirement instead. **Identifier hazard inside this same fix:** "r1" in the binding file means **P2-A** repair-1 (`superseded_p2_compiler_r1` = 1a3f20f8), while the P2-B repair is *also* called r1 (ea217b8d) — two different r1's, so the repoint must name the sha, never the suffix. Same family as D068. | Recorded | `docs/decisions.md` D071; `docs/status.md` §24 追加 31; dispatched to the verifier pre-seal. | D072 | **A rejection row's reason check must carry at least one key measured ABSENT from every foreign stream's `diag` surface, or the row must be declared reason-weak in its own detail — measured over 60 archived streams, every rejection row in `run_p2_suite_v3.py` (sha `3d804ce08e5ae8d9`) is satisfied by at least one foreign stream's diagnostic, and `t_a3`/`t_a4` are satisfied by ALL 27.** Method: the Lead imported the runner's own `primary_diagnostic`/`classify_lines` (zero transcription) and ran each row's key-list against each archived `*.stderr`. Measured accept-counts / 27 diag-bearing streams: `t_a3` (:994, keys `llvm,stage,late`) **27/27 = zero discrimination**; `t_a4` (:999, `aveintrin,regbaseintrins,stage,late`) **27/27**; `t_c4` (:2021, `l2_cache,cache_mode,invalid store,valid`) 20; `t_a5`/`t_a6` 9; `t_b12b` (:1807) 8; `t_b3` 8; `t_a14` 5; `t_b12c` 5; `t_a9` 4; `t_c3` 4; `t_a7`/`t_a8` 3; `t_b12a` (:1761) 2. **Why the matcher's own rule makes this the right surface:** `primary_diagnostic` skips every line whose `classify_lines` kind is not `"diag"` — dump bodies, attached IR notes and boundary/cascade lines are excluded as sufficient evidence — so a key free only in an IR dump or on the cascade line cannot make a row green; the discriminant is the `diag` surface alone, where `late` is free on **26** of the streams and `valid` on **20**. Only eight keys are free on none: `.bc`, `emit-llvm`, `lib dir`, `linked ir`, `meta_op`, `mismatch`, `template`, `version`. **Consequence for reading a result:** for `t_a3`/`t_a4` a PASS asserts nothing beyond `rc != 0` plus the absence of the fail-closed stub — the same "guard that fires on everything" shape as D043, now quantified rather than exemplar, and the census built to pre-empt that family does not report it. **Two defects in the census instrument itself** (`verify/work/p2b-acceptance/d057c-pre-run-census.py`): (a) its dump corpus is `embedded[:50] + embedded[-20:]` — 62 of 2488 lines — so it prints its "ok … only named-diagnostic corpora" verdict for `llvm` (447 hits in the full stream), `regbaseintrins` (106) and `version` (78) where its own untruncated rule yields COLLISION-RISK; (b) its collision rule credits `dump` and `boundary` kinds that the matcher excludes, so its ok/COLLISION-RISK verdicts are a *different predicate* from the one that decides a row. Both are fixed by enumerating the whole stream and classifying lines with the runner's own `classify_lines`. **Disposition:** apply the cross-matrix as a per-row negative control (D043's remedy, operationalised) — a row that accepts a named foreign stream is the row whose keys add no discrimination — then either strengthen that row's key list with a token measured against **its own** archived diag body and absent from every foreign diag surface, or record it as reason-weak; and correct the census's corpus and predicate. **Scope, stated to avoid over-claim:** the negative claim "absent from every foreign diag surface" is itself a check whose search space is the 60 archived streams of this suite (a complete enumeration of that directory, not of all possible runs), so it is a property of this frozen corpus and must be re-derived if the corpus grows. | D079 | **The P2-B source cut is a consumable: no P2-C (or other) source edit may begin while the four-file snapshot still carries the artifact of record's attribution.** The cut's acceptability rests on the Challenger's 1(c) condition, and the manifest that satisfies it (`lead/scratch-p2b-source-snapshot-20261014/P2B-SOURCE-SNAPSHOT.txt`) governs by a predicate, `exactly-four-modified-nothing-else`, over `regbase/BiShengIRCompileMain.cpp`, `regbase/LatePipeline.cpp`, `regbase/Utility.cpp`, `hivmc/A5/HIVMCMainA5.cpp` at base HEAD `cfaa59677a8b…` (diff `89d64743…`, 9987 bytes, 82+/7-). **A fifth modified file does not merely complicate the manifest — it breaks the chain that ties `stage-p2b-r1` `ea217b8d…` to a described cut**, and P2-B's acceptance rests on exactly that attribution. Two legal states and one illegal one: (i) the four dirty files, uncommitted, per the manifest as written; (ii) the cut committed, with the manifest **re-derived** against the new HEAD (a commit is authorized and is not itself a problem — it moves the base the manifest names); (iii) **illegal** — a fifth modified file alongside the four, which silently voids the attribution whether or not anyone notices. Corollaries carried into the order: the implementor holds all edits (`LatePipeline.cpp` is one of the four, so the P2-C delta — the §6.2 identity gate, the exactly-one `runHIVMCCompileA5`, removal of the fail-closed stub — is blocked until P2-B's acceptance is recorded), and the `build/bin` restore from `lead/preserved-p1-compiler/` stays the phase's last action (D041 ordering), with its before/after guard log taken at that moment. | **Recorded (Lead, P2-B gate)** | Measured `git status --porcelain` in `/home/s00653124/workspace/AscendNPU-IR-1` = **4** — the invariant is being held as of this row. Dispatched to the implementor 2026-10-14T21:09Z as a hold, with the P2-C plan-only task as the permitted substitute (no edits); the alternative to holding is state (ii), which requires re-deriving the manifest rather than reusing it. This row exists because the coupling is **not** derivable from §8's phase order alone: the order says P2-C follows P2-B, but only the manifest's predicate says *why an edit before acceptance is destructive*; a message would not survive the session, the register does. |
| D080 | **A summary is identified by its own run id, by ids that are a subset of the sealed registry, AND by a subject identity — and a summary lacking all three must not live at a path that reads as "the current run's summary".** Measured instance, found 2026-10-14 while pre-flighting the seal: `verify/logs/suite-v3/suite-summary.json` (1968 B, mtime 2026-10-14 04:11:20 +0800 = 2026-10-13T20:11:20Z, matching its own `utc`) carries the old key set `['config','utc','counts','results']`, `counts = {PASS:5, FAIL:0, BLOCKED:0, NOT_RUN:0}`, `config` = the bindings **path**, and result ids **`['N1','N2','N3','N4','P1']`, all five PASS**. `N1`–`N4`/`P1` occur in **none** of the current registry's 45 ids — it is definitively pre-rename — and those are the ids the plan reserves for the four **C310-bitcode negation strategies** (`docs/p2-verification.md:114-117`). So the default reading of that file is "the four C310 negatives passed", when the five rows were matcher **self-test controls** requiring no compiler and no build. **This is D068's predicted manufactured-confidence outcome, existing as an artefact rather than as a hypothesis** — and it sits at the **log root**, beside the per-run directories, which is the one path a reader guesses for "the summary". It also instantiates D077 twice: subject named only by path, and that path's contents changed twice the same day (9105 → 9723 → 11658 B). | **Recorded (Lead, pre-seal); the edit is the verifier's** | **Disposition ruled, mechanism left to the file's owner.** (a) **Do not delete it** — it is a real 20:11:20Z run's evidence and D025 forbids overwriting old evidence; retire it by **rename** so the filename carries its run and its id namespace (or move it into a run-named directory), so the path stops reading as current. (b) **The seal manifest must enumerate the summaries that belong to it and exclude this one** — ruling 3's "enumerated IDs (post S1-S4 rename)" is exactly the clause that catches a summary whose ids are absent from the sealed registry. (c) The generalised check, applicable to any future summary: keys lacking `selected`/`selection_mode` while the file sits outside a per-run directory is the shape to sweep for. **Why a row and not a note:** the three identification requirements are independent — the run id distinguishes runs, the id-subset requirement distinguishes id **namespaces**, and the subject identity distinguishes **artefacts**; a summary satisfying two of the three still misleads, and this instance satisfied none. Status update: `verify/logs/suite-v3/` held **three** scoped runs (20:40:01, 20:53:14, 21:05:44) at the time of measurement and **no full-registry run** against the bound r1 compiler, so the amended acceptance still needs one. |
| D081 | **A seal's self-description must satisfy its own re-check: a formula recorded beside a hash must be the formula that produces that hash.** Measured defect in the P2-B seal object: `SEAL-v3-MANIFEST.json:70` records `registry.snapshot_formula = sha256(json.dumps({'registry': ids, 'selected': null, 'selection_mode': 'full-registry'}, sort_keys=True))` next to `registry.snapshot_sha256 = 21d8d8ad869c52d8…`, but `21d8d8ad…` is produced only by the OTHER branch of the frozen runner's ternary — `selected=<the 7-element --only list>`, `selection_mode='--only'` (`run_p2_suite_v3.py:2740-2744`). Recomputing strictly by the recorded formula yields `4a31990c595e2e9b…`. The enumerated ids are correct and the true formula is on file inside the sealed runner, so the object stays re-checkable — but not by its own annotation. **Two harms:** (i) an independent re-checker who follows the recorded formula gets a mismatch and must conclude either tampering or a broken formula, which is precisely the failure a seal exists to prevent; (ii) the full-registry run still owed (Task 6) will emit the genuinely different `selected:null` snapshot (`4a31990c…` expected), so the present annotation would put **two different objects under one label** and read as the full run contradicting the seal. **Class:** the same family as D042 / the matcher-literal and summary-naming lessons — an annotation adjacent to a value that the value does not satisfy, which reads as verification while verifying something else (or nothing). Note what is NOT affected: every byte identity in the manifest re-hashes correctly (9/9 independently re-measured), the census is faithful (23/23 regexes and 15/15 key-lists re-derived and verbatim), and no PASS depends on the label. **Disposition:** versioned revision by the verifier (old bytes retained per D080's rename-not-overwrite rule), the corrected annotation naming the `--only` branch, and an explicit `supersedes` link; then Task 6, whose acceptance must state that 5 of the scoped run's 7 PASSes are compiler-free matcher controls. | **Recorded (Lead, P2-B post-seal invariant audit, 2026-10-14)** | Measured from the frozen set: `snapshot_sha256 = 21d8d8ad869c52d8db9810266f0253376b1c8d568376b405fa692fd49d018acd`; independent recomputation under `selected:list/--only` = `21d8d8ad…` (MATCH), under the recorded `selected:null/full-registry` = `4a31990c595e2e9b…` (NO MATCH); `snapshot_formula` occurs exactly once in the evidence tree, at `SEAL-v3-MANIFEST.json:70`; the runner's own formula is at `run_p2_suite_v3.py:2740`. | D082 | **A token seen in tool output may be a harness-masking placeholder that exists in NO file: never transcribe such a token into the durable record -- pin it by locator + length + sha256 instead.** During the P2-B full-run audit a `{{SECRET_XXXXXXXX}}` literal appeared inside `suite-summary.json`'s A13 detail as the *matched reason key*.
